D didn't know what they said, but before long, he saw the group of Psyducks crossing the river, following Koraidon to this side.

D's heart twitched, looking at the silly Psyducks in front of him, observing their state, seeing that they didn't seem troubled by headaches, he then asked, "Did you all understand?"

"Quack!"

Koraidon commanded, "Argh-gah-hoo!"

Instantly, the Psyducks started moving.

They swarmed into the field, and in no time, came out hugging a variety of fruits, piling them up on the grass.

Seeing them working so capably, D raised his eyebrows in surprise.

Learned so fast?!

He initially thought it was a good thing, but soon problems arose.

A Psyduck, in attempting to move a large watermelon, found itself lacking strength and decided to use telekinesis to move the melon.

But just as the light in its eyes began to glow, the Psyduck felt a sharp pain in its head, as if its brain was being squeezed.

"Quack!"

It cried out in pain, sitting heavily on the ground, clutching its head tightly with both hands, even drops of sweat trickling down its forehead.

Seeing this scene, D was startled, hurrying forward to check on the Psyduck's condition.

"It must be that using telekinesis stimulated its brain,"

After a brief contemplation, D said to Cyclizar next to him, "Help take care of these Psyducks and don't let them use telekinesis."

Saying this, D mounted Koraidon, rushing at top speed to the Pokemon Center in Porto Marinada Town to purchase a box of special medicine for Psyduck headaches from Miss Joy.

After feeding it to the Psyducks, they reverted back to their silly expressions.

D: "..."

He suddenly regretted his decision a bit.

Not only did the work not get done, but he even ended up spending money on a few bottles of special medicine.

Pressing his lips together, D let out a sigh, waved his hand at the group of Psyducks, and said, "Forget it, you guys go back and rest! Consider these six watermelons my treat."

Saying that, he summoned Dragonite: "Dragonite, come over and help me deliver the watermelons across the river!"

Dragonite immediately carried the watermelons to the other side of the river.

The Psyducks tilted their heads, their eyes drawn to Dragonite carrying the watermelons, and then they all hopped and skipped towards the other side of the river.

"Digletts are still the most obedient!" D sighed.

Hearing this, a few Dragonairs and Dratinis that had snuck over from Dragonite Island flew over.

"Woo!"

D: "...you're well-behaved too, but remember, don't go into the Diglett holes again or you'll get stuck and won't be able to come out."

Thinking of his own silly actions, Dratini was filled with shame and rage, turning away and ignoring him.

Dragonite chuckled watching the scene.

It was eager for these Dratinis and Dragonairs to hurry back to Dragonite Island.

So, seeing Dratini's demeanor, Dragonite hurried over to ask, "Awoo?"

If they want to return to Dragonite Island, it can help!

Dratini took a sneak peek at D, loudly saying, "Woo!"

It was saying, "I'm angry! I'm going home!"

However, unfortunately, D hadn't eaten translation nuts lately because of heartburn and couldn't understand Dratini's words.

Seeing no reaction from D, who was still working with Koraidon and Cyclizar, Dratini felt immediately aggrieved, seeming as if it might cry in the next moment.

Dragonite thought this wasn't good.

Sure enough, the next second, it saw Dratini flying over to D, with big, misty eyes.

"What's wrong?" D asked, somewhat surprised.

Dratini didn't say anything, instead, it flew onto his shoulder, wrapped its little body around his neck like a scarf.

D was charmed by Dratini's actions, giving its white big nose a kiss.

Seeing this, a few Pokémon nearby were shocked and envious.

Looking at the envious faces of Koraidon, Cyclizar, and Dragonite, D's heart skipped a beat, quickly changing the topic: "Let's get back to work! Otherwise, we won't finish in time!"

No kidding, he definitely wouldn't go around kissing big Pokémon.

Raising Pokémon is like raising kids; you can kiss them when they're small, but once they grow up, you just can't bring yourself to do it.

Though Cyclizar isn't even one year old yet, it's grown to an unignorable size.

What kind of dad would kiss a kid that's 1.7 meters tall?

Luckily, the workload was indeed heavy, so after a brief moment of disappointment, everyone returned to work.

Koraidon, Cyclizar, and Dragonite collected the crops to be harvested onto the grass, while D carried a basket and started picking hops.

He didn't plan to sell these hops, intending instead to store them to brew beer.

There were a lot of hops, enough to brew two batches of beer.

You could harvest hops about twice in a summer, just enough to save for brewing in the autumn and winter.

Besides, the wheat he planted to make malt was also ready for harvest.

D planned to harvest the hops before cutting the wheat, leaving the corn, pineapples, watermelons, and pumpkins to the farm's Pokémon.

During harvest, D kept a close eye on the surrounding crops, looking to see if there were any mutations like before.

Unfortunately, this batch seemed to only produce two mutated crops: heart's desire pumpkins and glowing pineapples.

Despite the regret, D felt that having two was already quite lucky given the odds.

So, when Professor Sada arrived at the farm with Arven, they saw Koraidon, considered a treasure by the research institute, busy with farm work.

Professor Sada seemed to witness something shocking, her face showing disbelief.

Previously, D had only said that Koraidon had a gentle temperament, but she never imagined that this Koraidon could be so docile.

In Professor Sada's mind, Koraidon, a Pokémon from ancient times, had always been wild, aggressive, fiercely dominant, and extremely possessive.

Yet here, it was helping D with farm work!

For a moment, Professor Sada was at a loss for words.

And to watch Koraidon work so skillfully, it was evident that it hadn't shied away from labor while living on the farm…

# 177: Chapter 174: Professor Sada and Arven (4k)

D also noticed Professor Sada and Arven not far away.

At first glance, he thought he was seeing things and stood there dumbfounded for a moment.

How could these two people appear here?

"Professor Sada?" D exclaimed in surprise.

Professor Sada nodded lightly, a spirited smile appearing on her face, "Long time no see, D."

"Why did you suddenly visit?" D stopped what he was doing and went forward to greet the guests.

Upon hearing this, Professor Sada glanced at Arven beside her, pondered for a moment, and said:

"Last time, when you mentioned those things, I found them reasonable. So this time, I specially took a few days off to relax with Arven."

D nodded, remembering that he mentioned last time to Professor Sada and Professor Turo that Arven mistakenly thought Koraidon and Miraidon had taken his parents away, which is why he didn't particularly like those two Pokemon.

Looking at Professor Sada's demeanor, it seemed like she wanted to make it up to Arven?

However... if you're looking to relax, shouldn't you be going to an amusement park to ride a carousel and ferris wheel? Why think of coming to a ranch?

D was puzzled.

Professor Sada saw his expression, seemingly reading his thoughts, and explained with a smile:

"Enjoying the idyllic scenery with Arven and experiencing rural life are great choices, plus…"

As she spoke, her gaze shifted to Koraidon, "We can simultaneously observe this Koraidon up close."

Ever since hearing D's words last time, the more Professor Sada thought about it, the more it made sense.

So she let her husband Turo watch over the lab while she took Arven out for a proper break.

Thus, they came to the ranch, ready to enjoy some farm life, and simultaneously observe the differences between this unique Koraidon and the one in Zone Zero.

D: "..."

D understood Professor Sada's meaning, and for a moment, didn't quite know what to say.

He looked at Professor Sada, then at Arven beside her.

After Professor Sada's words, Arven, who was initially quite cheerful, immediately became upset.

It was rare for his mother to take time off and go out with him, so Arven was originally very happy.

He eagerly anticipated the thought of his mother taking him to the amusement park for some fun and then having a big meal, maybe even a lively family gathering — just like other families.

But as it turned out, they came to this place, and when he noticed his mother focusing all her attention on Koraidon, Arven's previously upturned mouth turned into a straight line, and his young face showed a deep sense of disappointment.

D: "..."

"So, can we have a picnic here?" Professor Sada asked, "I'll cover the cost and rent for the space."

D: "..."

Well, since they're already here, he couldn't really send them away.

He glanced at little Arven, hesitatingly saying, "Of course, we can, it's just that the ingredients on the ranch are running a bit low..."

Professor Sada nodded, "Then I'll visit Porto Marinada Market. Arven, would you like to come along?"

Unhappy as he was, little Arven kept his head down and said nothing.

Seeing this, Professor Sada could only say: "In that case, you stay here and wait for me, I'll go to town and buy some of your favorite foods."

Having said that, Professor Sada turned and left.

D left the farmland, washed his hands, and then took a drink from the fridge to hand to Arven, who was standing still.

"You're called Arven, right? I heard your name from Professor Sada!"

Arven looked at the man in front of him and shook his head honestly, "I'm not thirsty."

"Then drink it when you're thirsty, just take it!"

Seeing no way to refuse, Arven mumbled a small thank you.

"Just call me D," D grinned, never expecting that senior Arven from the game would have such a side.

However, from the looks of him now, he seemed to be only seven or eight, not yet ten.

While waiting for Professor Sada to return, D had Arven wait there, while he took his Pokemon to the farmland to finish the remaining work.

By the time he had finished piling the harvested crops onto the lawn, Professor Sada had returned to the ranch with a large bag of ingredients, and she handed him a stack of League coins.

D counted it and found it amounted to a hefty sum of ten thousand League coins!

Wow, they're wealthy!

Since she had paid, he couldn't be careless.

D carefully inspected the ingredients Professor Sada had bought and decided to plan a lively and warm barbecue farm gathering for her and her son.

He prepared the necessary tools, cut the meat into small pieces, skewered them on iron skewers, and also made a few glasses of chilled watermelon juice.

"Do you need me to do the grilling?" D asked.

Professor Sada shook her head, "We'll do it ourselves!"

Seeing this, D decided to move to the other side, not wanting to disturb the mother and son's conversation.

However, he hadn't expected Professor Sada to call out to him, "Join us, there's plenty, and I and Arven won't finish it all. Tell me more about Koraidon!"

D thought for a moment and agreed, setting up a picnic blanket on the grass nearby with a bunch of Pokemon, and laid out the prepared sandwiches and snacks on it.

Koraidon, Cyclizar, Dragonite, Glaceon, and other Pokemon sat around the picnic blanket and began to feast with gusto.

Watching this, Professor Sada once again felt a sense of wonder.

---

She initially thought D was just an ordinary farmer, simply lucky enough to encounter an injured Koraidon.

Then he saved Koraidon, and out of gratitude, Koraidon stayed on the farm.

But now it seems... the young man before her is anything but ordinary.

She doesn't believe that an ordinary farm owner could raise such a large group of rare Dragonite, Dratini, and Dragonair.

Moreover, the Pokémon living on this farm all seem to be bred to perfection.

Robust Gogoat, Pawmo with sleek fur, the sturdy and hefty Naclstack...

The seemingly ordinary Pokémon should only be the Cyclizar, Butterfree, Miltank, Combee, Dolliv, and Alcremie, right?

But upon reflection, these Pokémon are often used for farm production in the Paldea Region; they aren't battle Pokémon, so such a situation is normal.

As Professor Sada grilled meat for Arven, she said, "Taking care of so many Pokémon must be hard, right?"

D smiled, glanced back at the Pokémon retrieving sandwiches from the picnic basket: "It's not too hard because they're all very well-behaved."

After a pause, D began to talk about what Professor Sada was most curious about.

"Especially Koraidon, it's not only very powerful but also helps me a lot in daily life, taking me to town, assisting in plowing fields, sowing seeds, helping me train Dragonite and Cyclizar..."

"Its personality is also quite endearing. It will get jealous of other Pokémon, and sometimes it forgets its own weight and tries to climb onto the bed, which results in the bed being broken by its weight."

"To be honest, since meeting Koraidon, I've already replaced two beds."

D recalled the events of recent times as he recounted them.

Professor Sada listened with great interest, even Arven standing by was captivated.

After all, the Koraidon that D spoke of was very different from the one he had seen before.

Professor Sada nodded: "This is quite normal. Because of their dominant and domineering nature, Koraidons have a very strong possessive instinct. It's normal for them to be jealous. Once you establish a bond with it, it becomes very dependable."

D nodded with a light smile: "Indeed!"

Professor Sada's face also showed a slight smile as she handed the grilled meat to Arven.

D saw Arven's tense little face relax as he began to enjoy the long-missed family affection.

"Seeing you like this, I can rest assured," said Professor Sada. "This at least proves that ancient Pokémon can survive in this era."

D: "..."

He suddenly felt that Professor Sada seemed a bit obsessed.

After a moment's hesitation, D curiously asked, "Professor, have you and Professor Turo always worked at the Paldea Giant Pit?"

Professor Sada nodded: "Yes, there's much worth studying there. The Terastal phenomenon familiar to Trainers outside was discovered by Turo and me from the depths of the Paldea Giant Pit."

"What's it like there?" D asked.

He had only seen it in the game; if placed in reality, the interior of the Paldea Giant Pit should be quite stunning, right?

Seeing his curiosity, Professor Sada smiled slightly: "The environment there is very special, with complex and diverse terrains—lush green grasslands, steep slopes, waterfalls formed by rivers, lakes, deep caves—almost every terrain you can see elsewhere is there."

"...In short, it's a place full of mystery and profoundly magnificent."

D sighed in admiration: "I'd love to see it!"

Professor Sada smiled: "There may be a chance, but not now."

The Paldea Giant Pit is full of various mysteries and unknowns. It was only 79 years ago that humans established an observation station in the Paldea Giant Pit, and its mysteries began to be unveiled.

But the terrain there is too complex and precipitous, and there are very dangerous Paradox Pokémon inhabiting it.

Thus, the Paldea League has regulations that only allow researchers to enter the Paldea Giant Pit. Traveling Trainers and Academy students are completely prohibited from entering during off-campus activities.

Hearing this, D was not surprised.

Thinking of the plot events to happen in the future, he couldn't help but cautiously inquire:

"In such an environment, doing research must be very dangerous, right?"

Professor Sada: "Those dangerous Pokémon usually don't come near the research facility. On the road, there's an observation station. That's a safe route to reach the research facility. As long as you pay a bit of attention, you won't encounter too much danger."

D intentionally said: "What about conducting research? The research institute must be full of all kinds of machines. In the movies, those research institutes often explode, causing the researchers inside to die."

"That's just in movies." Professor Sada couldn't help but laugh.

D: "..."

In his heart, he thought, you and Turo were killed in that explosion at the observation station, leaving Arven an orphan.

"It's safer to be cautious." D said, "Actually, I have a good method."

Professor Sada raised her eyebrow: "Oh?"

D seriously said, "That is, every research institute should keep a Pokémon with the Damp ability. With them, you might be able to prevent explosions."

"Damp ability?" Seeing D's serious look, Professor Sada was a bit surprised, but politely nodded: "Thank you for the advice. I'll pay attention when I return."

That was where he chose to end his warning.

Seeing this, D didn't continue further and shifted the focus to Arven.

"Arven must be turning ten soon, right?"

Speaking of her son, a tender look appeared on Professor Sada's otherwise energetic face: "Yes, he just had his ninth birthday, and this time next year, he'll be ten."

Witnessing this scene, D couldn't help but think:

No matter what happens in the future, or judge Professor Sada's dream of having ancient and modern Pokémon live together, she seems to truly love her son at this moment, right?

"Does Arven live with you at the research facility?" D asked again.

Professor Sada shook her head, looking a bit guilty: "No, the work at the research facility is quite busy. Most of the time, Arven stays near Cabo Poco Town. When he turns ten next year, we'll send him to the Paldea Academy."

In the afternoon, Professor Sada left with Arven.

D stood with Koraidon and Cyclizar in front of the farm, watching them leave.

Watching the young Arven, D seemed to see a giant gear appear above his head.

At this moment, the gears of destiny began to turn slowly, and Arven would embark on a future of his own.

"It could... be changed, right?" D murmured to himself.

Seeing D standing there in a daze, Koraidon tilted its head and looked over: "Ah gah-sis?"

At this moment, he hadn't eaten the Translation Nuts, so he couldn't understand Koraidon's words, but based on its tone and expression, he could roughly guess its meaning.

It was probably asking: "What are you thinking about?"

After organizing his thoughts, D replied: "I'm thinking about what their future will be like."

Future?

Koraidon glanced at the two far away, then silently said: "Ah gah-sis..."

"Forget it, I won't think about it anymore!" D couldn't figure it out and chose to stop pondering. Anyway, it couldn't be worse than the game's plot.

He laughed and patted Koraidon's head, saying: "Let's head back and tidy up. Zack should be coming to collect the goods later. Once we make some money, we'll go find Keri tomorrow to build a big house, so we don't have to squeeze into this small cabin anymore!"

D turned and walked back to the farm, looking at the little wooden cabin.

Previously, he hadn't thought it through enough. Now it seemed that if he planned to build a Pokémon Tavern, this wooden cabin might not need to be demolished.

The big house could be built elsewhere. This cabin, with some renovations, could become a small tavern.

Just move out the furniture, install a wooden bar countertop, and lay out a few small tables for Pokémon. The tavern could officially start operating.

But the problem is, would there really be Pokémon customers coming to the tavern to 'drink'?

Even with Mew and Teddiursa helping spread the word outside, those areas and the Paldea Region are separated by a vast open sea.

It's manageable for Pokémon that can fly or swim, but for those that can't fly and have no means to cross the sea, it's problematic.

"Let's consider it later!"

After a brief reflection, D made a decision in his heart—

If a Pokémon customer visits the tavern in the next period, he will officially start the business.

If not, then he'll abandon this plan and convert the cabin into a barn for storing grain.

# 178: Chapter 175: Our Home

At five in the evening, Zack was getting ready to come to the farm to pick up the goods.

D helped him load the vegetables and fruits onto the truck.

Seeing the rather excited expression on D's face, Zack looked very surprised, "Why are you so happy all of a sudden? Did something good happen?"

D nodded, "Tomorrow I'll finally have enough money to build a house, and I won't have to squeeze into this small log cabin anymore!"

"Oh, that is indeed something worth celebrating," Zack grinned. "In that case, let me congratulate you in advance!"

After a brief chat, Zack drove away in his large truck.

D returned to the cabin, placed the money he earned today on the desk, and took out the savings he had accumulated over the past few months from a drawer.

"...Hmm, including the money in my bank card, I now have 2,153,400 League currency on hand. Two million will be used for building the house, and the rest will be reserved for purchasing some furniture and household items."

D silently calculated the next steps in his heart.

Not only does building a house cost money, but I'll also need a considerable amount for furniture and such.

Besides this, he also needs to buy a batch of barrels to place in the cellar for brewing wine.

And there's one more thing.

If he plans to operate a Pokemon Tavern, it also needs a bit of renovation, with a bar set up and tables and chairs customized for different Pokemon sizes.

When the Indeedee Sibling comes, he can have the brother Indeedee help entertain guests at the tavern.

Just then, several Pokemon who had been playing outside all day ran into the house.

Seeing D dazing in front of the desk, they all showed puzzled expressions.

A group of Pokemon gathered around, making questioning growls.

"Graaaw?"

D explained, "I'm thinking about the new house. Tomorrow morning, construction of our new home can begin. It'll be a more spacious house with various rooms and a living room, and your activity area will become larger."

Upon hearing this, the eyes of the Pokemon brightened.

Alcremie exclaimed in delight, "Ma-ma?!"

A bigger house? Could it possibly have a Cream Workshop of its own?

D guessed Alcremie's thoughts, as he had promised Alcremie this before.

"That's right, I'll reserve a separate room to be converted into a Cream Workshop. I'll place an oven, cream buckets, and similar kitchen utensils there, and it'll be dedicated to making desserts," D said.

"Ma-ma!"

Alcremie's eyes shimmered with anticipation and longing.

Seeing its expression, D smiled and turned to the other Pokemon, "What about you? What kind of house do you like?"

While asking, he took out a bowl of peeled Translation Nuts from the cabinet.

Cyclizar was the first to respond, "Graaw!" (A warm room!)

"A warm room?" D recalled Cyclizar's aversion to cold and understandingly nodded, "No problem. When the time comes, we can install a fireplace in the living room. Lighting it in winter will ensure the whole house is warm."

"Graaw~" Cyclizar let out a happy growl.

Next, D turned his attention to Dragonite.

As it was its turn, Dragonite cheered, "Awoo!" (I want a very big room! And I want a lake to swim in, sunbathe, and watch the stars!)

D: "..." Quite demanding!

Actually, Dragonite prefers being outside compared to indoors.

Initially, it spent every night in its Poke Ball, but gradually, as Dragonite became more familiar with the farm, it started sleeping on the grass outside, rarely coming indoors to sleep.

D started thinking about what kind of room could meet Dragonite's needs.

Unfortunately, such a room doesn't exist in this world.

He poked Dragonite's big belly and said mercilessly, "There's no such room in this world. You might as well sleep on the grassy field outside! There's plenty of space, a lake, and you can see stars and sunbathe, perfectly meeting your requirements."

Dragonite felt dejected. So there's no such room?

Oh well, it might as well continue sleeping on that soft grassy field outside! Every night, it can still gaze at the beautiful stars and moon.

"What about Koraidon?" D looked at Koraidon.

Compared to Dragonite, Koraidon was much more laid back, "Ah-gaaah!" (Sleep in your room!)

D: "..."

He should have expected this answer.

Jotting down Koraidon's preferences in his notebook, D turned to Pawmo, "What about you, Pawmo?"

Pawmo chirped with excitement, "Ba-mo!" (Sleep with D!)

"Alright." D patted Pawmo's head, then glanced around, "So that's settled then?"

The Pokemon had no objections.

Early the next morning, D went to the town to find Keri.

Learning of his intentions, Keri was very surprised, "So soon?"

D smiled and nodded, "Yes, and it's all thanks to Miltank and Gogoat."

"Alright, I see." Thinking of those illogical Gogoats, Keri suddenly wasn't too surprised anymore.

She turned and led the way, "Follow me!"

With Keri leading, D arrived at a room that was sort of like a workshop.

The room was a mess, with various blueprints strewn across the table and floor.

Keri walked to the table and pulled out a few blueprints from a drawer and handed them over.

As D reached out and took them, he saw drawings of two slightly different houses.

One was a fairytale-like Scandinavian countryside cottage, and the other resembled the house of Nobita Nobi from Doraemon, a Japanese-style two-story residence.

"Choose one!" Keri said.

D's eyes moved back and forth over the two blueprints, ultimately resting on the fairytale-like countryside cottage.

The reason was simple; it looked very much like the house from Stardew Valley. It seemed more pleasing and fitting for the farm.

Seeing his choice, Keri nodded with a smile, "Indeed, this kind of residence is more suitable for the farm. In that case, let's decide on the interior layout!"

"Generally speaking, when building, I'd plan the rooms in advance: bedroom, living room, kitchen, bathroom, dining room on the first floor; side bedroom, nursery, and study on the second floor. There are also two empty rooms for you to use as storage, and under the house is a cellar, which can be used for winemaking or converted into a basement."

"Besides these, let me know if you have any other requests?"

D relayed the results of the discussion with the Pokemon to Keri.

After hearing, Keri nodded, "A Cream Workshop... a room for making desserts, that's easy. Besides that, anything else?"

D shook his head, "That's all."

Keri: "The materials and construction fees total 1.8 million, which includes costs for flooring, carpets, and wallpaper, which you can choose."

"Additionally, our store offers custom furniture services. Besides basic tables and chairs, you can also buy sofas, cabinets, beds, various lights, and so on."

Altogether, the estimated cost will be around two million.

D had anticipated this, so he stayed calm and nodded, "I understand. Just regular wooden flooring, and wood-patterned wallpaper will do."

"No problem," Keri smiled. "I need to prepare a bit. We'll start construction tomorrow morning, and it will likely take one to two months."

# 179: Chapter 176: The Visitor

Since this was about building their new home together, both D and the Pokemon looked especially hopeful and attentive.

Therefore, when Keri was working with Machamp and Conkeldurr, D often stood watching from a distance, observing them lay foundations, saw wood, mix cement...

They anticipated the completion of their new home, where they would start a brand-new life.

For D, this new home had extraordinary significance.

It was not just a house but a shared home with the Pokemon, a product of their hard work, and the entire ranch was filled with an atmosphere of joy.

Amidst this atmosphere, time unknowingly advanced to May 19th.

That evening, Keri and several Machamp stopped their construction work, and covered in dust and mud, she walked up to D.

Keri: "By the way D, are you planning to participate in the fishing competition tomorrow?"

Thinking about how he had been practicing his fishing skills these past few days, D nodded: "If nothing unexpected happens, I'll participate."

"Then you should do your best!" Keri said, "I heard Allen is also participating, and his fishing skills are quite impressive. People in town often see him walking around with a big fish."

D was a bit puzzled: "Why haven't I heard him mention it?"

"Didn't he say so?" Keri seemed equally surprised. She thought for a moment and added, "Maybe he wants to surprise you?"

D: "..."

Well, let's talk about it tomorrow!

It's getting late. Today's construction is over. Keri took Machamp and Conkeldurr and left the ranch; they planned to take the morning off tomorrow to watch the fishing competition.

D had no objections, even though he wanted to move into the new house as soon as possible, he wasn't in too much of a hurry.

After all, having waited so many days, a little longer wouldn't hurt.

Watching Keri's silhouette as she left, D turned to prepare dinner for the Pokemon.

To be in the best condition for tomorrow's fishing competition, after dinner, D went to bed early.

He initially thought he would wake up early the next day, head to the exciting fishing competition, and win first place.

However, plans couldn't keep up with changes.

Early the next morning, just as D was about to set off for the competition venue at Lake Verity, an unexpected guest arrived at the ranch.

Staring at the young girl who appeared in the ranch like a phantom, D stood frozen.

She was an extremely beautiful young girl dressed in a kimono with pink cherry blossom petals printed on it, her long black hair elegantly tied up, revealing a flawless swan neck.

The girl looked over with ethereal eyes, appearing so fragile as if her life force was fading away.

Suddenly, she lifted her foot and walked over frivolously on what seemed like quiet flames.

D's gaze was instantly captured by this incredible sight.

At this time, Cyclizar, prepared to take him to the fishing competition site, looked over curiously when D remained stationary for a long time: "Gaao?"

D glanced at Cyclizar, but realized it seemed not to see the girl walking towards them at all.

D was slightly taken aback and then widened his eyes in disbelief: "C-Cyclizar, can you see the person over there?"

Cyclizar followed his pointing direction but saw nothing.

Can't see? Could it be a ghost in broad daylight?

D shivered, and suddenly, a strange voice echoed in his mind: "This is an illusion technique."

Stunned, the girl walking on fire suddenly erupted into a flame.

Within the flame, the girl's form kept changing and finally disappeared, replaced by a four-legged Pokemon with a fox-like appearance.

It was cloaked in glimmering gold fur, with nine fluffy and slender tails swaying gently as it walked. Its crimson eyes showed no expression, and its proportionate body conveyed nobility and elegance.

It's... a Ninetales?!

D's eyes opened wide.

At this moment, Cyclizar could finally see the suddenly appearing Ninetales, and it jumped in fright as well.

"Gaao?!"

Hearing the commotion, Koraidon, Dragonite, and other Pokemon ran over. Upon seeing the situation, they curiously directed their gazes toward the unexpected Ninetales.

Ninetales seemed unconcerned about Dragonite and the other Pokemon, only looking calmly at D.

"Teddiursa and Mew told me that a Pokemon Tavern opened here, where Pokemon can fulfill their wishes."

Teddiursa and Mew?

Hearing these names, D immediately understood the Ninetales was here for the Pokemon Tavern!

Though the tavern wasn't constructed yet, it wouldn't stop him from starting the business.

After a brief thought, D asked: "Do you have a wish you hope to fulfill?"

Ninetales remained silent, and a glimmer flashed in its crimson eyes.

Soon, D witnessed the scene around him rapidly change.

The ranch disappeared, replaced by an ancient mansion.

A kimono-clad girl, showing anxiousness, rushed into the mansion carrying a wounded Vulpix, shouting at the householder: "Please save this Vulpix!"

D saw the Vulpix was injured.

Responding to her plea, the mansion's owner agreed and healed Vulpix's injuries.

Since then, Vulpix began living with the girl, growing up with her, slowly maturing until it reached the end of its life.

The girl's hair turned white, and Vulpix became Ninetales.

It lingered long at her graveside, unwilling to depart, until the world underwent drastic changes: seas turned into land, the sun rose and set, forests grew, and the gravestone vanished in time.

After witnessing the illusion crafted by Ninetales, D couldn't help but feel moved.

He asked, "Is this your wish?"

Gazing at the human across, Ninetales nodded gently: "My life is nearing its end. Before it vanishes, I wish to see her one last time."

D: "..." Although he wanted to help Pokemon fulfill their wishes, he wasn't a wish-granting machine, nor could he make the Time God Dialga send this Ninetales back in time.

Seeing his expression, a wave of disappointment swept through Ninetales' eyes: "Is it impossible?"

D: "…Actually, I am just a normal human."

Hearing this, Ninetales did not speak further, turned around, and attempted to leave.

Watching its silhouette retreat, D's mind quickly raced, and suddenly, he recalled a dish he had once made.

—A caramel-flavored cream fruit cake!

The cake's effect is a sweet dream that temporarily allows Pokemon to forget their troubles and sorrow and meet who they want to see in the dream.

Thinking of this, D hurriedly called out: "Wait!"

Ninetales turned back in puzzlement.

"Is it just to see her once?" D asked, "No matter the way?"

Ninetales nodded slightly.

D: "If that's the case, follow me then, this matter I can manage."

# 180: Chapter 177: The Tavern's First Guest

Today's plans were disrupted by the unexpected arrival of Ninetales. D glanced at the time, realizing he might not make it to the fishing competition.

So he first used the video phone to inform Mayor Thomas that he couldn't attend, then began dealing with the matter of this Ninetales.

Ninetales lightly raised its four paws and followed inside, its nine golden, fluffy large tails swaying gently behind it, looking dreamlike and somewhat unreal.

Behind it, Cyclizar, Koraidon, Dragonite, Alcremie, and other Pokemon also gathered around, curiously gazing at this mysterious Pokemon.

"Do I need to do anything?" Ninetales asked.

Based on its understanding, places like human-run restaurants, taverns, and cafes usually required payment.

Money...

It seemed it didn't have any.

This left Ninetales feeling a bit embarrassed, and it started pondering how to acquire money.

D understood what this Ninetales meant.

According to his original plan, the tavern was free of charge.

Of course, if Pokemon wanted to pay, they could offer anything from a Berry, a normal stone, or even a fresh flower, and he would gladly accept it.

From the illusion technique just displayed by Ninetales, its strength must be very formidable.

Since it had telepathy, he didn't need to eat Translation Nuts.

D thought for a moment and thoughtfully said, "No need, you just have to wait for a moment."

Ninetales breathed a sigh of relief and gently nodded, "Thank you."

Hearing this, D didn't say much more, but turned around and started to check the ingredients in the fridge.

The steps to make that dessert were somewhat complicated, requiring ingredients like Alcremie's cream, cake, and berries.

Fortunately, he hadn't made cakes much during this period, so the fridge was well-stocked.

Putting what he needed on the kitchen table, D turned to see Ninetales crouched on the ground, waiting.

Looking at this scene, D couldn't help but think to himself, "It really suits the tavern's operating style."

He began to imagine the scene of the tavern operating.

The kitchen table transformed into a bar counter, a red carpet laid over the wooden floor at the entrance, the fireplace burning brightly, the room warm, and several wild Pokemon drinking and playing in the tavern.

And he, in turn, became a bartender dressed in a suit vest and white shirt, wiping a glass while watching the lively scene before him.

This was a typical medieval tavern style, only replaced with Pokemon, which didn't seem out of place.

Realizing he still had tasks to accomplish, D refocused his mind and began earnestly making the dish.

As time passed by the second, Ninetales patiently crouched there, waiting.

Its gaze was fixed on the busy human, recalling the moment when Teddiursa and Mew found it not long ago.

They claimed to be from the Heroic Expedition Team, saying they were traveling the world and asked why it was sad.

After learning the reason, those two Pokemon showed a sad expression, but they were helpless in the matter and could only turn away in sorrow.

Ninetales watched their figures disappear. It planned to fall asleep, but unexpectedly, a few days later, the two Pokemon suddenly returned.

They excitedly said that in a distant land, across the sea, there was a magical Pokemon Tavern.

There lived the kindest human in the world, possessing magical abilities to help Pokemon fulfill various wishes.

Ninetales still remembered the expression on Teddiursa's face when it spoke these words.

It excitedly and enthusiastically said, "D can help you! Go find him!"

Thus, it came.

To see an old friend's face and hear her voice one more time, Ninetales, which normally wouldn't cross the sea, traversed mountains, lakes, and the ocean, arriving at the other side, at this magical little tavern.

D called over Alcremie, instructing it to make fresh caramel cream and evenly spread it over the cake base.

With his actions, a cream cake emitting a sweet fragrance gradually took shape.

Initially, when making it, the kitchen tools were still incomplete, so the cakes produced were rather simple.

Now, with complete ingredients and tools, the cake looked even more refined.

The final step was to cut the Berry into pieces and decorate the cake.

"Done!"

D looked down, and the details of this brand new cream cake immediately appeared in his mind.

[Cream Fruit Cake (Caramel Flavor) (B): A fruit cake made with Alcremie's hand-whipped cream. Its delicious cream and beautiful appearance make it more attractive.

Cooking Effect: Sweet Dream lv2, allows Pokemon to temporarily forget worries and sadness, to have a sweet dream, and see the people and things they wish to see in the dream, a dessert blessed by the Dream God.

Evaluation: A dessert that brings happiness to both humans and Pokemon.]

"As expected, using higher-grade ingredients also raised the dish's level," D thought to himself.

He remembered that last time its level seemed to be D-?

Also, the cooking effect upgraded from lv1 to lv2, with the description now including "blessed by the Dream God."

Dream God... In D's mind appeared a vision of a beautiful figure, seemingly clad in pink gossamer, like a fairy.

---

That is Cresselia, the Dream God, the embodiment of the new moon, and the counterpart to Darkrai.

A legend circulates in the city—it's said that if you sleep with Cresselia's feather, you'll be protected from nightmares and have a beautiful dream.

Just from the description, D couldn't tell what more the sweetness of a level 2 dream offered compared to level 1.

After a brief moment of thought, he cut a small piece of cake and placed it in front of Ninetales.

Catching the aroma coming from the cake, Ninetales was slightly startled, a look of doubt surfacing in its bright red eyes.

"Wanna try it?" D asked.

Ninetales had no appetite and didn't want to eat: "I just want to see her once."

D: "Eating this will let you see her, though in the form of a dream."

Upon hearing this, Ninetales said no more.

With a bit of suspicion, it lowered its head, held onto a try-it-out mentality, and ate the cake in front of it noncommittally.

"Take a nap!" D lamented that the cake did not induce sleep immediately; they would have to wait for the Pokemon to naturally fall asleep for it to work.

Ninetales blinked, thinking of the question this human asked it earlier.

"You just want to see her once, no matter the way?"

At this moment, Ninetales suddenly realized what that way meant.

Sure enough, the following words from the other confirmed its guess.

"This cake can let you dream of the person you want to see."

Ninetales remained silent.

Dreaming? This wasn't a bad idea.

"You can sleep here; don't worry, it's safe," D said.

"Thank you."

Ninetales expressed its gratitude, then turned around to go outside, its eyes gazing into the distance. It then ran, with flames underfoot, to a tree, lay beneath it, and closed its eyes to sleep.

Ninetales initially thought it would have trouble sleeping, but listening to the mumbling sounds of nearby Pokemon and feeling the rhythm of the wind, unconsciously, a wave of drowsiness washed over it.

When it opened its eyes again, it was surprised to find itself in the ancient mansion from its memories, a time already over 1500 years ago.

Ninetales looked around, finding everything so familiar, almost unchanged from its memory.

Its long silent heart was suddenly moved, as if a drop of clear water fell onto a dusty floor.

A hint of anticipation silently emerged in Ninetales' heart.

Then, the door of the house was pushed open, and a young girl in a kimono, wearing wooden clogs, smiled as she walked up to it.

"So you ran here!" the girl said gently: "Won't you get sunburned? Come over here and rest!"

Next, Ryoko picked it up, walked to a big tree in the yard, sat under its shade, and began reading a book.

Ninetales didn't mind reverting inexplicably to Vulpix; it snuggled in the girl's embrace, the name Ryoko surfacing from deep within its memory.

Ryoko...

How long had it been since it heard this name? Ninetales couldn't remember anymore.

The afternoon sunlight scattered across the ground, the girl sitting under the tree reading, and Ninetales quietly accompanying her.

Was this a dream?

But why did this dream feel so real? It could even smell the scent of the surrounding plants.

This thought flashed through Ninetales' mind, but it didn't ponder further, standing up and jumping onto the girl.

The girl seemed to understand its intention, smiling as she grabbed Ninetales' two forepaws, placing it on her lap, and gently scratched its nose:

"Mischief! Alright, sleep here!"

Ninetales didn't want to sleep.

The girl frowned slightly: "Can't sleep? That won't do! Later, we'll be going to the forest to gather herbs, and without energy, it's easy to miss the herbs growing in the thickets!"

Upon hearing this, Ninetales immediately closed its eyes.

The girl finally smiled: "That's more like it! Good Vulpix!"

But just then, she noticed something wrong: "Oh? Why are you suddenly crying?"

"Woof!"

Ninetales quickly blinked its eyes, pretending it just got some dust in them.

"Got something in your eyes? Let me blow it out for you!" The girl leaned in and blew gently a few times: "There, it's gone now, you can sleep peacefully!"

Ninetales nodded slightly, then closed its eyes, curling up on the girl's lap, quietly enjoying the atmosphere.

Even years later, it would still remember this afternoon, this warm afternoon that brought it comfort.

In the pasture, D was observing Ninetales through the bubble above its head.

He noticed that when Ninetales cried in the dream, a tear also rolled down its cheek in reality.

A few other Pokemon gathered around, also seeing Ninetales' dream.

Cyclizar's face showed sorrow: "Grrrr..." (So sad...)

D hadn't finished eating the bowl of sunflower seeds he had this morning, so he naturally understood Cyclizar's words.

Being companions who had lived together for a long time, D understood Cyclizar's meaning even without explanation.

"Indeed, but isn't it just the way things are?" he said.

"Grah-scree?" (What do you mean?)

Seeing the Pokemon looking over simultaneously, D continued:

"Because different species inherently have different lifespans! Birth, old age, sickness, and death are common occurrences for people and all creatures in the world face this one day; it's just a matter of sooner or later."

Dragonite quickly asked, "Roar?" (Does that mean D will also die?)

Hearing Dragonite's words, the heads of all the Pokemon drooped.

"Grrrr..." (I don't want D to die...)

Koraidon looked terrified; though it came from the future, it wasn't a very distant future.

It couldn't imagine that day...

As far as it knew, humans were fragile, and their lives were short; some only lived for a few years or decades, with few making it to a hundred.

What to do? So scared, what if D ends up like those short-lived ones and dies tomorrow?

The more Koraidon thought, the more uneasy it became, finally asking directly: "Gra-scree?!" (D, how much longer can you live, it won't be only ten years or so, right?)

Upon hearing this, D developed a headache: "Why are you jinxing me like that?!"

Koraidon quickly realized it misspoke and covered its own mouth.

D understood their thoughts; they were touched by what happened to Ninetales.

After some thought, he said spiritedly: "Don't worry, for your sakes, I'll strive to live till I'm two hundred!"

Dragonite pressed on for details: "Roar?" (What about after two hundred?)

The other Pokemon also wanted to know the answer and eagerly looked over.

D coughed a couple of times and said: "Maybe I'll become a Pokemon! I once heard that some Pokemon are the result of humans turning after they die. Have you ever seen a Gengar?"

He recalled watching a short Pokemon animation in his previous life, where one episode was titled "Did I become a Gengar?"

The protagonist of that episode turned into a Gengar while she stayed at school, and with the help of another Gengar, she overcame her social anxiety.

Later, keen-eyed viewers suggested that the Gengar helping the girl was likely her sister.

Because of its smile and the charms on its phone, hinting subtly.

For this reason, some speculated that Gengar might indeed be humans turned into Pokemon after death.

Although this was just an urban legend with no truth, nothing would be surprising in this world.

"Therefore, as long as you remember me and don't stop thinking of me, there might be a miracle; if I become a Gengar, I'll find you wherever you are."

D smiled, reaching out to pat the heads of all the Pokemon in front of him one by one: "Then, don't fail to recognize me!"

Considering he was able to traverse to this place, it was already a miracle in itself.

Though these words lacked logic, the pure and innocent Pokemon believed it.

They definitely wouldn't! No matter what D became, they'd recognize him!

---

---

---

---

# 181: Chapter 178: Explosive Popcorn (4k)

Ninetales slept for a long time.

In its dreams, it cried and laughed, its mood sometimes elated, sometimes sad, as if it had returned to that beautiful, youthful time.

D and a group of Pokemon watched with mixed emotions.

Seeing the beautiful girl in a kimono grow into an old woman with wrinkles, gray hair, and a stooped back, they couldn't help but feel nostalgic.

...No wonder Ninetales was so sad, crossing mountains and seas just to see that person again.

However, this seemed to fulfill one of Ninetales' wishes.

When it woke up, its face had returned to a calm and silent expression, but compared to before, there was now a hint of gentleness.

It got up from under the tree, swinging its nine tails that shone brightly under the sunlight, and solemnly expressed its gratitude to D.

"I will remember this day forever," it said.

"It's actually not a big deal," D quickly shook his head and waved his hands, "I'm just glad to help you."

Then he asked, "Where are you headed next?"

At this, Ninetales looked towards the distance.

It didn't say explicitly, just responded, "To where I ought to go."

D was slightly surprised, then nodded, "Alright, then we wish you a smooth journey."

The only pity is that the tavern hasn't been built yet, so as the tavern's first guest, Ninetales might not be able to see it.

Thinking about this, D couldn't help but feel a sense of regret.

Ninetales looked at the human in front of it with gratitude and nodded slightly.

Then it raised its head, let out a melodious cry, treaded on flames, and headed towards the horizon, eventually disappearing from everyone's sight.

Gazing in that direction, D felt sentimental.

But when he turned around, he saw the tearful faces of the Pokemon on the ranch.

D: "..."

The Dratini and Dragonair had never seen a Pokemon like Ninetales, nor met a Trainer of their own, nor felt that kind of deep emotion.

Yet this did not prevent them from being moved.

The three Dratini and two Dragonair had tears glistening in their eyes.

They were both moved and envious, moved by the deep affection between Ninetales and the human, and envious that Ninetales had met such a good human.

Thinking of themselves, these five still-growing Pokemon looked pitifully at D, wanting to know if they would end up like that too.

"Woo?"

D thought for a moment, wanting to say something.

But before he could answer, the Dragonite next to him cracked its knuckles loudly.

Hard, the knuckles were hard.

"Roar!"

With a serious expression, it stepped forward to take away these five obstructive juniors, telling them that D was its Trainer and that they could only be treated well by it alone!

D: "..."

He could only say, "What's most important in life is to learn to live in the moment, enjoy the now."

The few Pokemon listened with seeming understanding.

D shrugged helplessly: "Don't you think the future is too distant? If you live every day worrying about the future, wouldn't all that time be wasted?"

"A day of happiness is a day, a day of unhappiness is also a day, so why not live cheerfully every day?"

"So, don't worry about the future, I promise you all that each one of you is unique to me," D said sincerely.

For some reason, he suddenly felt his words sounded a bit like a cliché from a flirt.

Because no matter the creature, they all feel jealousy, just like the cats and dogs he had raised in his previous life.

Though Pokemon are smarter, they can't escape this aspect.

Now, as long as they know each one of them is different to him, it's enough, since that is D's genuine thought.

Sure enough, after hearing this, the group of Pokemon all showed moved expressions.

D: "..." No, he needs to work on developing some longevity cuisine!

*

Ninetales departed.

The ranch returned to its usual lively atmosphere, as though it had never appeared.

Because of Ninetales, D missed this year's fishing competition.

He heard from Allen about the fishing competition aftermath; the Champion title was taken by a dark horse uncle who emerged suddenly.

It's said that this uncle, wearing a red and white hat, caught twenty-eight Magikarp in just four hours, clinching the Champion title of this year's fishing competition.

Nicknamed the Magikarp Uncle, after fishing, the Magikarp Uncle gleefully continued his travels.

As for the absence of this fishing king, the townspeople all felt regret.

D: ...Lucky he didn't go, otherwise it'd be embarrassing.

Early the next morning, Keri continued the construction at the ranch.

While D started a new round of crop planting.

Today, taking advantage of the good weather, he planned to expand the farmland area a bit and then plant more.

So early in the morning, D went to the Diglett Hole and shouted a few times inside.

After a while, a small Diglett popped its head out of the hole, looking at him somewhat puzzled.

"Squeak?"

D smiled, "Today I have something to ask of you."

Upon hearing this, Diglett's eyes brightened instantly.

Ever since it was hired by D, it has been living in the underground cave with the group of Naclstack, and every day it gets to eat the food made by D.

---

Diglett was quite grateful, always conscientiously awaiting the boss's call, eager to offer its modest help.

But it had waited for a long while, and D hadn't come to ask for its help, yet it continued to eat D's food.

This made Diglett feel very ashamed, as if it was freeloading.

So, upon hearing there was something to do today, Diglett immediately became excited.

"Squeak squeak!"

Seeing Diglett's expression, D raised an eyebrow.

Does this little Diglett really enjoy working that much?

However, for him, this seemed to be a good thing.

Thus, D led Diglett to the plot of land he planned to develop and said to it:

"It's here, I'd like to ask you to till this land. I plan to plant more crops here."

Diglett understood immediately.

It eagerly dived into the ground, moving quickly like a small bump.

The land tilled by Diglett quickly became loose at a visible speed, exuding the fragrance of soil.

Before long, a neat field appeared in front of D.

Looking at this scene, D felt very satisfied.

From this perspective, the work efficiency of a Pokemon like Diglett is quite high, no wonder it's welcomed by farmers.

"Well, next is to set up the supports, and plant the hops." D was secretly planning in his mind.

The last batch of hops had been harvested and stored by him, reserved for brewing later.

But the quantity of those hops was still somewhat insufficient, so D planned to expand the planting scale.

Approaching noon, Diglett had just finished tilling the entire field.

Plus, with the hot weather, it was already sweating profusely. Seeing this, D fed it a bottle of Berry Milk, then let it rest in the cool underground first.

Diglett obediently agreed, then dived into the underground tunnel.

Meanwhile, D returned to the house, glanced at the old-fashioned wall clock, and, seeing that it wasn't early anymore, decided to make lunch first, planning to set up the supports for the hops in the afternoon.

Today's lunch was still sandwiches and Pan-Fried Pork Chops paired with Berry Milk, but because of the hot weather, D added cooling foods like chilled watermelon and creamy shaved ice to the menu for the Pokemon.

It's worth mentioning that he also experimented with making dishes from the pumpkins, pineapples, onions and other fruits and vegetables he had previously harvested.

However, the Cooking Effect of those dishes turned out not to be very ideal.

Without the variability in size and the luminescent effect, the dishes made from ordinary pumpkins and pineapples only had effects of restoring stamina and replenishing energy.

This made D feel very regretful; he wondered if his cooking technique was incorrect.

But since he had sold all the crops to save money for building a house after harvesting, keeping only one or two of each, the dishes made from them couldn't serve as references.

"Maybe I can carefully try again when the next batch of crops matures." D pondered silently.

The afternoon sun was becoming more dazzling, the weather outside was scorching, and D really didn't want to work at this time, so he stayed in the air-conditioned room with the Pokemon watching TV.

A food program was on TV, where the host was teaching the audience how to make a snack very popular among children—popcorn.

Looking at this scene, D suddenly remembered that the corn in his field was also about mature.

He noticed the Pokemon around him were being attracted by the popcorn on the screen, which made him a bit speechless.

He fed them well every day, so why are they still so greedy...

But perhaps they had never tried popcorn before?

Just as he had nothing to do after the meal, D went to the cornfield, picked a few ears of corn, had Koraidon, who was stronger, rub off the kernels, washed them, and then started making them according to the food program's tutorial.

First step, heat the pot and pour in some oil, when the oil was hot, he put in the corn to stir-fry a bit.

Once some of the kernels started to pop, D quickly covered the pot, and then a muffled popping sound came from within.

The sound startled the Pokemon behind him, and they all gathered curiously to watch the pot.

The ordinary corn kernels kept expanding and finally popped into golden popcorn.

The sound grew louder, vaguely resembling a string of firecrackers being set off in the pot, rapid and dense.

But soon, the sound gradually decreased, and all the corn kernels disappeared, turning into fragrant, full popcorn.

He lifted the lid, and the "little chubbies" sprang out eagerly from the pot.

Seeing this, D suddenly realized he had put in too much corn!

He hurriedly used a basin he had prepared to catch them, pouring all the popcorn from the pot into it.

He couldn't help but glance at the fragrant popcorn.

Immediately, information about these popcorn appeared in D's mind.

[Explosive Popcorn (B): A simple snack made from corn, paired with soda, it's the perfect combination when watching a movie.

Cooking Effect: Explosion Specialization lv1, upon being eaten by a Pokemon, the explosion-type moves used by the Pokemon will be strengthened, enhancing the power of the moves.

Evaluation: A very nice snack, quite popular among children.]

"Huh? Explosion Specialization?"

D quickly began recalling all explosion-related moves in his mind.

Starting with the most famous, Self-Destruct…

This is a move that nearly takes both parties down, causing an explosion that attacks all surrounding Pokemon, but the Pokemon would be in a near-faint condition after use.

Then there's the enhanced version, Explosion, Head Charge, Steam Eruption.

If moves with "explosion" in the name count, then add Blast Burn, Metal Explosion, and Mew's Z-move, Genesis Supernova...

Though it sounds useful, it wasn't very applicable to the Pokemon around him.

Wait!

Not all were useless.

D suddenly remembered the description of Koraidon's exclusive move.

Collision Course: Transform while fiercely descending, causing an ancient explosion, further increased in power if hitting a weak spot.

So the question arises, can this move be classified as an explosion-type move?

Only one way to find out.

Thinking about this, D handed the steaming hot popcorn to the Pokemon.

Once he saw Koraidon eating it, he mysteriously called Koraidon outside.

"Agrr?"

Koraidon, who followed him outside, looked puzzled.

D led it to a grass field and said, "Can you show me Collision Course once?"

Koraidon blinked, not understanding why D suddenly wanted to see this move, but still demonstrated it for him.

D stood still, holding his breath.

He saw Koraidon spread its arms, leap into the air, its vibrant feathers transformed into wings, and then, while changing shape, it fiercely descended towards the ground.

"Boom!"

A huge explosive sound came, and D felt the earth tremble a bit, a buzzing sound echoed in his ears, and a large cloud of dust obstructed his view.

Not realizing, D inhaled it, coughed twice, and nervously looked towards Koraidon.

A moment later, the dust cleared, and aside from Koraidon, there was also a gigantic crater in front of him.

Koraidon stood in the crater, with a face full of confusion and puzzlement.

What happened? It clearly held back its strength?

After seeing the scene in front of him clearly, D was taken aback, his vision went black, and he almost fainted on the spot.

His freshly trimmed lawn!

Dammit, he underestimated the destructive power of a Legendary Pokemon!

He couldn't be blamed, after all, a Pokemon like Koraidon was too deceptive.

Who would have thought this Pokemon, similar to Cyclizar, is a cover legend like Groudon and Kyogre?

"Hold on, hold on." D forced himself to focus on Koraidon's move: "It seems your Collision Course was enhanced by the popcorn just now."

"Agrr?!" Koraidon was first shocked, then slowly realized: "Agrr."

---

---

---

---

# 182: Chapter 179: Summer Festival

At 2 PM, Keri arrived at the ranch and immediately noticed a huge crater on the lawn.

Her face showed a look of astonishment: "Was this hit by a meteorite?"

D: "..."

Next to him, Koraidon hung its head low; it wasn't intentional.

Seeing this, Keri was even more surprised: "Did Koraidon do this? It's so powerful!"

"I asked Koraidon to demonstrate a move for me." D said helplessly, "I didn't expect the move to be so powerful."

He was pushing a small cart loaded with dirt, which he and Koraidon just dug from the mountain to fill the crater.

D tipped over the cart, dumping all the dirt into the big hole, making several trips before the land was finally somewhat leveled.

However, the turf here was blown away by Koraidon's Collision Course, leaving it bare in stark contrast to the surroundings.

D thought for a moment, turned around, and took out a bag of Pasture Seeds, scattering it evenly on the ground, then called over the Gogoats lounging under the pergola, asking them to help take care of this patch of grass.

To get the grass to grow quickly, as it doesn't look good being bald.

This task was very simple for the Gogoats; they immediately surrounded the area and began infusing it with Grass Energy.

"Whew!"

D patted one of the Gogoats on the head, then turned to see Keri starting to get busy with several Machamp.

Lately, they had already dug a square pit and laid out the cellar outline with moisture-proof materials and boards.

Keri was busy over there, and D had no plans to idle.

The sunflower heads from the last harvest had all been cut down by him and were placed under the sun to dry. Then, several Pokemon helped shell the dried sunflower seeds.

The sunflowers from three plots produced a total of five large boxes of sunflower seeds.

With nothing better to do now, D moved these boxes to the kitchen and began roasting the sunflower seeds, preparing to vacuum pack them as Translation Nuts for gradual consumption.

By the time D finished roasting all five boxes of sunflower seeds, May 31 had arrived.

That day, the summer festival was held.

At 8 PM, people would gather at the beach on the west side of Porto Marinada Town to watch the fireworks display.

"Hey, D, don't forget to watch the fireworks tonight!" Zack reminded while moving several barrels of milk onto a truck.

"I remember." D naturally hadn't forgotten; he was quite looking forward to it, having only seen such festivals in anime before.

Like the Ho-oh Festival in Johto Region's Ecruteak City, where there are singing geishas and plays depicting the legends of Ho-oh and Lugia.

"That's good." Zack grinned. He wanted to say more but then noticed D's blue workwear: "Wearing that is not appropriate for a summer festival!"

D was slightly taken aback.

Zack: "You should wear traditional festival attire!"

D: "..." Could he say he wasn't prepared?

"Judging by your look, you haven't prepared yet, have you?" Zack laughed, "At this time, the shops in town are probably closed. How about this! I have a set at home. It's my nephew's, he never wore it, I'll deliver it to you later!"

D shook his hands repeatedly: "No, no, I'll just change into clean clothes later."

Zack frowned: "How can that do? This special day comes only once a year; you must take it seriously!"

D: "...Alright then."

"Now that's the spirit!" Zack enthusiastically slapped his shoulder: "Just wait, I'll bring it to you later!"

Watching Zack's figure fade into the distance, D went back inside, took a quick shower, and changed into clean clothes.

Then he gathered all the Pokemon at the ranch: "Who wants to join me for the summer festival?"

"Woo!"

Several lively and mischievous Dratini and Dragonair responded immediately.

"Great!" D nodded in approval and looked at the other Pokemon.

Koraidon and Cyclizar were naturally willing to go, and Dragonite and Pawmo as well.

Alcremie, Naclstack, Glaceon, and Butterfree wanted to join the fun, too.

In the end, all the Pokemon at the ranch were put into Poke Balls, including Combee and Miltank.

Only Koraidon, Dragonair, and the Dratinis remained outside.

As D looked at the heavy load of Poke Balls in his pocket, he felt a bit lost.

He hadn't noticed how many Pokemon he had caught!

He placed these Poke Balls into his Space Backpack. Then he turned his gaze to the Dratini and Dragonair: "Come inside? Let me see how you sneak into my backpack."

"Woo~"

The three Dratini and two Dragonair looked up with bright eyes.

They slipped into the Space Backpack one by one, and after the last Dragonair went in, it skillfully pulled the zipper with its tail.

However, the zipper wasn't fully closed, leaving a gap through which the Pokemon peered at D outside.

D monitored the situation of the Space Backpack.

From the outside, the Space Backpack did not appear bulging due to those Dragonair and Dratini inside, suggesting the interior space was much larger than he had imagined.

---

Even when lifted, it doesn't feel heavy. I must say, this space backpack is a remarkable piece of technology.

At this moment, a horn sounded outside the ranch.

D looked up and saw Zack jumping out of the car, carrying a package as he walked over.

"Here you go, put this on! It's already half-past six, we can set off now!"

D reached out to take the package. As he opened it, he found a gray kimono inside.

"I'm not going with you. My wife and kid are still waiting for me at home!" Zack grinned, waved at D, and then turned to leave the ranch.

D: "..."

He returned to the room a bit helplessly and put on the kimono.

Wearing this kind of clothing for the first time, D felt a bit uncomfortable, but after a while, he got used to it.

"Let's go!" He took a deep breath, slung the space backpack over his shoulder, hopped onto Koraidon, feeling both excited and expectant inside.

"Ah, gya-seeh!"

Koraidon responded, starting to run toward Porto Marinada Town.

By the time they reached the beach, quite a few people and Pokemon had already gathered there.

At this point, the sky was completely dark, and people were in groups, sitting or lying down, chatting with their families and Pokemon.

Not far away, a street full of brightly lit stalls appeared, with various items displayed on them.

There were steaming hot takoyaki, wooden carved souvenirs, and masks of various shapes.

Seeing this scene, D raised his eyebrows slightly.

Since there was still some time before the fireworks festival started, he decided to take Koraidon for a walk around the area.

The surrounding crowd surged like waves.

There were young children holding their parents' hands, a youthful boy with Pikachu on his shoulder, an old man with graying hair and a hunched back alongside a Dachsbun, and a bright-eyed girl with her Gengar staring at the takoyaki…

Amidst the lights, D and Koraidon also merged into this lively atmosphere.

D stopped in front of the mask-selling stall.

On the stall, he saw a variety of masks.

There were Pikachu, Eevee, Demon Fox Zorua, and even a Squirtle mask.

Seeing a customer arriving, the stall owner beamed: "These masks suit you very well. Would you like to buy one? They're only 2000 Poké Dollars!"

2000...

The price wasn't too high, so D simply paid, choosing the Zorua mask.

D put the mask on his face, turned around, and asked Koraidon, "How do I look?"

Koraidon squinted with a smile, looking very pleased: "Ah, gya-seeh~"

"It suits you very much!" the owner complimented from the side.

D smiled, and just then a vendor's shout caught his attention from afar.

He led Koraidon through the crowd and came across a middle-aged man with a red cap at a stall.

On his table was a large transparent fish tank, with a group of dazed Magikarp swimming slowly inside.

Noticing someone approaching, the man immediately shouted:

"Hey there, would you like one? My Magikarp are all top-tier Pokemon! You won't regret buying one!"

D felt both familiar and foreign with such situations.

Familiar because he's seen sellers of Magikarp in anime, but foreign because this was his first time seeing one in reality.

It made D quite curious: "What makes your Magikarp special?"

Hearing this, the man beamed and said:

"Of course! These are extremely rare Magikarp! I've carefully selected each one, with very high potential. Once they evolve into Gyarados, they'll help you take down Gym Leaders and kick the Elite Four's butts!"

D twitched his mouth, but before he could speak, a Trainer holding a Pawmi chimed in:

"That's too far-fetched, sounds like a scam. How could such Magikarp exist in the world?"

Being called a scammer didn't fluster the man at all. He simply smiled and said, "If you don't believe me, buy one and try it at home! See if it can evolve into Gyarados!"

"No way!" The boy made a face, turned around, and ran off.

The man fumed silently: "Brat!"

D: "..."

At that moment, the man noticed the backpack on the young man in front of him.

Through the gap in the zipper, he saw several pairs of eyes curiously observing him.

Upon realizing who those eyes belonged to, the man gasped and exclaimed in disbelief: "Dratini!"

D was slightly startled but then realized the Dratini in his backpack had been discovered.

The man's eyes burned with excitement: "So many Dratini! Where did you catch them?"

"I didn't catch them," D shook his head. "They followed me on their own."

"No way?!" The man was dumbfounded: "These are rare Dratini! I'm willing to pay a high price for one, will you sell?"

"I won't sell," D refused without hesitation.

"Then how about a trade?" The man suggested, producing a Poke Ball.

With a flash of white light, a Kingdra appeared in the water tank in front of him.

"I'll trade you my Kingdra for a Dratini, is that okay?"

"Not okay." D flatly refused: "Forget it, I absolutely won't trade."

The man felt crestfallen, watching the other's retreating figure with envy in his eyes.

"I can't believe there are people trading Pokemon here." D couldn't help but comment to Koraidon, "What do you think goes on in their minds?"

Koraidon: "Ah, gya-seeh…"

"Forget it, let's not dwell on them." D turned his backpack around, unzipped it, and let the Dratini and Dragonair inside take a breather.

After strolling for a bit longer, D felt a bit tired. He bought over a dozen servings of takoyaki and took Koraidon to a spacious, less crowded part of the beach.

"Everyone, come on out!"

He took out Poke Balls, and with flashes of white light, Pokemon appeared on the beach.

Pokemon like Alcremie, Butterfree, Glaceon, and Cyclizar had never seen sand before, so soon they started playing wildly on the beach.

D spread out a picnic blanket from his backpack, placing the takoyaki and berries he brought on it. He turned and reminded:

"Don't wander too far, just play around here! Koraidon, Gogoat, please look after them for me."

"Ah, gya-seeh!"

"Meee~"

Meanwhile, other passersby walked by.

A little girl was enthralled by the many Pokemon there.

"Wow! Mom, look over there!"

The woman, who was chatting with her husband, immediately turned around at the sound of her daughter's voice.

Then, they witnessed a jaw-dropping scene.

"So many Pokemon!" the woman exclaimed, half-jokingly thinking this guy must have brought his whole family.

"Big brother, are all these Pokemon yours?" the little girl asked naively.

D smiled and nodded, "Yes, they are!"

"Wow, that's amazing!" The little girl's eyes were full of determination: "When I grow up, I want to be a powerful Trainer and capture lots and lots of Pokemon!"

"Good luck!" D encouraged her.

As they still needed to buy food, the little girl couldn't stay long. She held her mother's hand and waved goodbye to D: "Bye big brother!"

"Goodbye!"

D took off the Zorua mask, turned, and saw that Cyclizar, Glaceon, and Dragonite had already started building sandcastles.

Three Miltank were also nearby, playing in the sand with great excitement.

Combee and Butterfree weren't very interested in the sand, so after playing for a while, they returned to D, landing on the picnic blanket.

D asked Koraidon: "Don't you want to go play with them?"

"Ah, gya-seeh," Koraidon replied dismissively. It's not a kid anymore.

D picked up on Koraidon's meaning, recalling its usual demeanor and couldn't help but laugh.

Koraidon: "..."

D: "Only kids sleep with adults."

Koraidon realized the teasing tone in D's voice, feeling both embarrassed and infuriated, it protested: "Ah, gya-seeh!"

"Isn't it true?"

Of course not!

Koraidon's head drooped pitifully, its crest wilting as well.

It simply... just wanted to be with D all the time.

---

---

---

---

# 183: Chapter 180: Fireworks

"Hahaha!"

Seeing Koraidon's appearance, D couldn't help but laugh.

In Koraidon's incredulous expression, D patted its head.

"Alright, I was just teasing you!"

He shifted his position, leaning against the grassy-scented wool of the Gogoat behind him, and thought back over the events of these past days, sighing with a sense of nostalgia.

Koraidon tilted its head to look at him for a moment, then leaned in closer and lay down on the sand like a big golden retriever.

Neither of them spoke again; they simply enjoyed the peaceful and beautiful atmosphere.

In the distance, Porto Marinada Town was brightly lit, and the waves gently lapped against the shore, leaving behind white foam.

The cries of Pokemon and the chatter of humans drifted from the bustling streets, carried by the evening breeze into the distance.

For a moment, even the wind seemed gentler.

Cyclizar, Dragonite, Pawmo, and Glaceon were playing with sand not far away, along with a few Dratini and Dragonair, while Naclstack and Miltank watched from the side.

Gogoat remained by D's side, leisurely munching on Berries from the picnic basket.

Dolliv sat on the picnic cloth, watching Butterfree and Combee chat softly nearby.

Occasionally, people would pass by, and every time they saw so many Pokemon, they would exclaim in surprise.

A few young Trainers, around the age of ten, were even drawn towards them.

D watched as they observed Dragonite and the others for a while before approaching him.

"Let's have an exciting Pokemon battle!" A youngster in shorts challenged him.

Looking at the excited expression on the opponent's face, D raised an eyebrow and then turned his head to ask, "Who wants to give it a try?"

The Gogoat stood up enthusiastically.

"Baa!"

With a wave of D's hand, the eager Gogoat approached the youngster in shorts.

A few seconds later, the youngster's Paras was sent flying.

"Amazing!"

The other watching Trainers gasped in unison.

At that moment, one of them seemed to recall something and asked, wide-eyed, "Excuse me, are you Mr. D?!"

Surprised to suddenly hear his name, D turned his head to see a boy in a light kimono looking at him with excitement.

"You know me?" he asked.

The boy nodded: "You're the founder of the Grass Trial! I heard stories about you at school!"

The boy's companion looked confused: "Grass Trial? What's that? A new Gym test?"

"Not at all!" the boy explained. "It's a trial to help Pokemon and Trainers train! They say those who participate in the trial see their skills greatly improve!"

"Really? Is there such a thing?" His companion was very surprised.

"Of course! I've always wanted to try it..." The boy showed regret: "But I haven't collected four Gym Badges yet, so I'm not qualified to challenge it, but I'll work hard!"

D thought: It seems the news about the challenge restrictions he set up had spread after the last three challengers.

"A lot of people know now?" he asked.

The boy nodded: "Yes, there's a legend about the three Gogoat at school! Many want to challenge it, but we're just freshmen and haven't collected four badges yet."

Hearing this, D suddenly understood.

No wonder there hadn't been any new challengers at the ranch recently, it was because of this reason.

"It seems the news has only spread among the lower grades," D thought.

The boy observed his expression and then looked puzzled: "Mr. D, I have a question for you..."

"Go ahead," D replied understandingly.

The boy: "Is there a non-disclosure agreement for challenging the Grass Trial? Carter and the others never reveal any details about the trial, and whenever we ask, they always have a very peculiar expression."

That expression was as if they were both pained and happy.

D: "..."

He smiled politely: "There's no non-disclosure agreement. You'll find out the details of the challenge when you come and try it."

Hearing this, the boy's face was full of anticipation: "I will definitely challenge it! We won't disturb you any longer, goodbye!"
